{\n    return M_1_SQRTPI * M_SQRT1_2 * exp(-0.5 * x * x);\n}\n\n// Black-Scholes formula for European options\ndouble blackScholes(const OptionInput& option) noexcept {\n    const double S = noexcept {\n    return 0.5 * (1.0 + erf(x * M_SQRT1_2));\n}\n\n// Standard normal probability density function\n__host__ __device__ inline double normalPDF(const double x) noexcept {\n    return S = option.spot;\n    const double K = option.strike;\n    const double r = option.r;\n    const double q = option.q;\n    const double T = option.t;\n    const double sigma = option.vol;\n\n    if (T <= 0.0 || sigma <= 0.0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n    const double d1 = (log(S / K) + (r - q + 0.5 * sigma * sigma) * T) / (sigma * sqrt(T));\n    const double d2 = d1 - sigma * sqrt(T);\n\n * exp(-q * T) * Nd1 - K * discount * Nd2;\n    } else { // PUT\n        price = K * discount * cumulativeNormal(-d2) - S * exp(-q * T) * cumulativeNormal(-d1);\n    }\n\n    return price;\n}\n\n//    const double computed = results[i];\n        const double expected = options[i].value;\n        const double error = fabs(computed - expected);\n        const double relError = error / Volatility\n    double value;       // Expected value (for validation)\n    double tol;         // Tolerance\n};\n\n// Standard normal cumulative distribution function\n__host__ __device__ inline double cumulativeNormal(const double x) noexcept {\n    return 0.5 * (1.0 + erf(x * M_SQRT1_2));\n}\n\n// Standard normal probability density function\n__host__ __device__ inline double normalPDF(const double x) noexcept {\n    return M_1_SQRTPI * M_SQRT1_2 * exp(-0.5 * x * x);\n}\n\n// Black-Scholes formula for European options\n__host__ __device__ inline double blackScholes(const OptionInput& option) noexcept {\n    const double S = option.spot;\n    const double K = option.strike;\n    const double r = option.r;\n    const double q = option.q;\n    const double T = option.t;\n    const double sigma = option.vol;\n\n    if (T <= 0.0 || sigma <= 0.0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n    const double d1 = (log(S / K) + (r - q + 0.5 * sigma * sigma) * T) / (sigma * sqrt(T));\n    const double d2 = d1 - sigma * sqrt(T);\n\n    const double Nd1 = cumulativeNormal(d1);\n    const double Nd2 = cumulativeNormal(d2);\n    const double discount = exp(-r * T);\n\n    double price;\n    if (option.type == CALL) {\n        price = S * exp(-q * T) * Nd1 - K * discount * Nd2;\n    } else { // PUT\n        price = K * discount * cumulativeNormal(-d2) - S * exp(-q * T) OptionInput option = table.opt[i % TestOptionTable::count];\n\n    // Add some variation for larger datasets\n    const double factor = 1.0 + 0.1 * (i / static_cast<double>(TestOptionTable::count));\n    option.spot *= factor;\n    option.strike *= factor;\n\n    return option;\n}\n\n// Prices a contiguous range of options [globalStart, globalStart + n) on the device.\n__global__ void priceOptionsKernel(const TestOptionTable table, const size_t globalStart,\n                                   const size_t n, double* __restrict__ out) {\n    const size_t stride = Hand every node-local rank a disjoint slice of the node's\n    // CPUs and place the threads there explicitly.\n    const int totalCpus = std::max(1,              coresPerRank, numDevices);\n    }\n\n    const TestOptionTable table = makeTestOptionTable();\n    const RankRange range = computeRange(numOptions, rank, numRanks);\n\n    // Pinned staging buffer for this rank's slice of the result vector: written directly\n    // by the CPU worker threads and by the asynchronous device-to-host copies.\n    double* localResults = nullptr;\n    if (range.count > 0) {\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaHostAlloc(&localResults, range.count * sizeof(double),\n                                 cudaHostAllocDefault));\n    }\n\n    cudaStream_t streams[kNumStreams] = {};\n    double* deviceBuffers[kNumStreams] = {};\n    const size_t gpuGrabElems = kChunkSize * kGpuChunksPerGrab;\n    const size_t deviceBufElems = std::min(gpuGrabElems, {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cf2xmNttrpxqyPqMNf6Qr","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. `black-scholes/black_scholes.cpp` and `black-scholes/CMakeLists.txt` now hold an unconditional MPI + OpenMP + CUDA implementation; executable name and CLI are unchanged.\n\n**Design**\n\n- **MPI** — ranks split the option index space into contiguous slices (`computeRange`). `MPI_Comm_split_type(MPI_COMM_TYPE_SHARED)` gives the node-local rank, which selects the GPU (`localRank % deviceCount`) and a disjoint slice of the node's CPUs. Results are collected with `MPI_Gatherv` on rank 0 (outside the timed region, like the original which timed only the pricing loop); the reported time is an `MPI_Allreduce(MAX)` over ranks.\n- **CUDA** — a kernel *generates* each option from its global index and prices it, so no option array is ever built or transferred; only the 7-entry base table travels as a kernel argument (constant memory) and only results come back (8 B/option) via async D2H over 4 streams into pinned memory.\n- **OpenMP** — the host cores are a first-class worker, not just a driver: a shared atomic chunk counter feeds thread 0 (which pumps GPU batches) and all other threads (which price chunks with libm). The split self-balances with no tuning; GPU grabs are large but taper near the end of the range so host threads don't idle on a final big batch. Threads are pinned explicitly with `pthread_setaffinity_np` — Open MPI binds a rank to a single core by default, which otherwise confined the whole team to one CPU (35× loss).\n\n**Measured** (4× RTX 3090, 2× EPYC 7763; serial baseline 11 M options/s):\n\n| n | 1 rank | 2 ranks | 4 ranks |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 50 M | 1.87 G/s | 2.50 G/s | 3.91 G/s |\n| 200 M | 2.35 G/s | 2.93 G/s | **4.36 G/s** |\n\n≈400× over the serial version at 200 M options; also faster than serial at the 10 000-option default (0.42 ms vs 0.93 ms). 8 ranks (2 per GPU) is slower, so one rank per GPU is the intended launch.\n\n**Correctness / semantics** — the formula, generation rule, argument handling, output format and exit codes are byte-for-byte the original ones; `-v` regenerates just the 10 inspected options locally. Verified against a serial reference build for n ∈ {0, 1, 7, 10 000, 1 000 001, 20 M} × {1, 4, 8} ranks.\n\nOne deviation to flag: CUDA's `erf`/`exp` differ from glibc's by ~1 ulp, and the call/put subtraction amplifies that to at most **2.8e-13 relative** on ~8% of elements (worst case measured over 20 M options). Sum/Min/Max/Samples therefore agree with the serial run to ~13–16 digits, but the FNV hash printed by `-r` differs whenever any element is GPU-computed, and — because the CPU/GPU split is dynamic — which elements those are varies between runs. If your validation compares that hash bit-exactly rather than comparing values with a tolerance, tell me and I'll make the split static and deterministic (the hash would then be stable per rank/thread count, though still not equal to the serial one — that is unavoidable with any GPU math library)."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":406,"cache_read_input_tokens":84231,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":406},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b5afda1e-5c76-4852-a28a-f439746ab8cd","uuid":"b4d0c698-c56c-414a-8211-88366623239c","timestamp":"2026-09-14T07:47:36.812Z","request_id":"req_011Cf2xmNMtZ1LwGXZTMR5nH"}
